.get_alnstats_for_bam Internal function to get seqlengths, mapped and unmapped counts for
a bam file
Description
Internal function to get seqlengths, mapped and unmapped counts for a bam file
Usage
.get_alnstats_for_bam(bf)
Arguments
bf Character scalar with the path and filename to a bam file (index is assumed to
be already existing)
Value
a named numeric vector with elements ’seqlength’, ’mapped’ and ’unmapped’
4 alignmentStats
alignmentStats Get statistics on alignments
Description
Get statistics on alignments from bam file or qProject object.
Usage
alignmentStats(x, collapseBySample = TRUE)
Arguments
x the source of alignment bam files, one of:
• a character vector with bam files
• a qProject object
collapseBySample
If TRUE and x is a qProject object, sum counts for bam files with identical
sample names.
Details
Internally, alignmentStats queries the bam index files similar to ’idxstats’ from samtools. Please
note that this does not discriminate for example between primary and secondary alignments. If you
need more statistics, see for example quickBamFlagSummary from package Rsamtools.
If x is a qProject object, the auxiliary bam files will not contain any unmapped reads, and the
corresponding unmapped counts are calculated by subtracting auxiliary mapped counts from the
total reads. The latter correspond to the unmapped counts from the corresponding genome bam
files.
Value
A matrix with one row per bam file and three columns ("seqlength", "mapped" and "unmapped").
Author(s)
Anita Lerch, Dimos Gaidatzis and Michael Stadler
See Also
qProject, quickBamFlagSummary from package Rsamtoo

qAlign Align reads
Description
Create read alignments against reference genome and optional auxiliary targets if not yet existing.
If necessary, also build target indices for the aligner.
Usage
qAlign(
sampleFile,
genome,
auxiliaryFile = NULL,
aligner = "Rbowtie",
maxHits = 1,
paired = NULL,
splicedAlignment = FALSE,
snpFile = NULL,
bisulfite = "no",
alignmentParameter = NULL,
projectName = "qProject",
alignmentsDir = NULL,
lib.loc = NULL,
cacheDir = NULL,
clObj = NULL,
checkOnly = FALSE,
geneAnnotation = NULL
)
Arguments
sampleFile The name of a text file listing input sequence files and sample names (see ‘De-
tails’).
genome The reference genome for primary alignments, one of:
• a string referring to a “BSgenome” package (e.g. “"BSgenome.Hsapiens.UCSC.hg19"”),
which will be downloaded automatically from Bioconductor if not present
• the name of a fasta sequence file containing one or several sequences (chro-
mosomes) to be used as a reference. The aligner index will be created when
necessary and stored in a default location (see ‘Details’).
auxiliaryFile The name of a text file listing sequences to be used as additional targets for
alignment of reads not mapping to the reference genome (see ‘Details’).
qAlign 9
aligner selects the aligner program to be used for aligning the reads. Currently, only
“Rbowtie” and “Rhisat2” are supported, which are R wrapper packages for
‘bowtie’ / ‘SpliceMap’ and ‘hisat2’, respectively (see Rbowtie-package and
Rhisat2-package packages).
maxHits sets the maximal number of allowed mapping positions per read (default: 1). If
a read produces more than maxHits alignments, no alignments will be reported
for it. In case of a multi-mapping read, a single alignment is randomly selected.
paired defines the type of paired-end library and can be set to one of no (single read
experiment, default), fr (fw/rev), ff (fw/fw) or rf (rev/fw).
splicedAlignment
If TRUE, reads will be aligned using a spliced aligner, depending on the value of
aligner described above:
aligner="Rhisat2" : This is the recommended setting for spliced alignments
and will use hisat2 from the Rhisat2-package. See also the geneAnnotation
argument below for providing known exon-exon junctions.
aligner="Rbowtie" : This is not recommended and only available for legacy
reasons. It will use SpliceMap to produce spliced alignments (without us-
ing a database of known exon-exon junctions). Compared to the alternative
alignment modes (non-spliced or spliced using Rhisat2 as aligner), this
alignment mode is about ten-fold slower and also less sensitive. Further-
more, SpliceMap can only be used for reads with a minimal length of 50nt;
SpliceMap ignores reads that are shorter, and these reads will not be con-
tained in the BAM file, neither as mapped or unmapped reads.
snpFile The name of a text file listing single nucleotide polymorphisms to be used for
allele-specific alignment and quantification (see ‘Details’).
bisulfite For bisulfite-converted samples (Bis-seq), the type of bisulfite library (“dir” for
directional libraries, “undir” for undirectional libraries).
alignmentParameter
An optional string containing command line parameters to be used for the aligner,
to overrule the default alignment parameters used by QuasR. Plea

 file(s) containing

6 preprocessReads
truncateStartBases
integer(1): the number of bases to be truncated (removed) from the beginning
of each sequence.
truncateEndBases
integer(1): the number of bases to be truncated (removed) from the end of each
sequence.
Lpattern character(1): the left (5’-end) adapter sequence.
Rpattern character(1): the right (3’-end) adapter sequence.
max.Lmismatch mismatch tolerance when searching for matches of Lpattern (see ‘Details’).
max.Rmismatch mismatch tolerance when searching for matches of Rpattern (see ‘Details’).
with.Lindels if TRUE, indels are allowed in the alignments of the suffixes of Lpattern with
the subject, at its beginning (see ‘Details’).
with.Rindels same as with.Lindels but for alignments of the prefixes of Rpattern with the
subject, at its end (see ‘Details’).
minLength integer(1): the minimal allowed sequence length.
nBases integer(1): the maximal number of Ns allowed per sequence.
complexity NULL (default) or numeric(1): If not NULL, the minimal sequence complexity,
as a fraction of the average complexity in the human genome (~3.9bits). For
example, complexity = 0.5 will filter out sequences that do not have at least
half the complexity of the human genome. See ‘Details’ on how the complexity
is calculated.
nrec integer(1): the number of sequence records to read at a time.
clObj a cluster object to be used for parallel processing of multiple files (see ‘Details’).
Details
Sequence files can be in fasta or fastq format, and can be compressed by either gzip, bzip2 or xz
(extensions .gz, .bz2 or .xz). Multiple files can be processed by a single call to preprocessReads;
in that case all sequence file vectors must have identical lengths.
nrec can be used to limit the memory usage when processing large input files. preprocessReads
iteratively loads chunks of nrec sequences from the input until all data been processed.
Sequence pairs from paired-end experiments can be processed by specifying pairs of input and
output files (filenameMate and outputFilenameMate arguments). In that case, it is assumed that
pairs appear in the same order in the two input files, and only pairs in which both reads pass all
filtering criteria are written to the output files, maintaining the consistent ordering.
If output files are compressed, the processed sequences are first written to temporary files (created
in the same directory as the final output file), and the output files are generated at the end by
compressing the temporary files.
For the trimming of left and/or right flanking sequences (adapters) from sequence reads, the trimLRPatterns
function from package Biostrings is used, and the arguments Lpattern, Rpattern, max.Lmismatch,
max.Rmismatch, with.Lindels and with.Rindels are used in the call to trimLRPatterns. Lfixed
and Rfixed arguments of trimLRPatterns are set to TRUE, thus only fixed patterns (without IUPAC
codes for ambigous bases) can be used. Currently, trimming of adapters is only supported for single
read experiments.
preprocessReads 7
Sequence complexity (H) is calculated based on the dinucleotide composition using the formula
(Shannon entropy):
H = −
X
i
fi log2 fi,
where fi is the fraction of dinucleotide i from all dinucleotides in the sequence. Sequence reads that
fulfill the condition H/Hr ≥ c are retained (not filtered out), where Hr = 3.908 is the reference
complexity in bits obtained from the human genome, and c is the value given to the argument
complexity.
If an object that inherits from class cluster is provided to the clObj argument, for example an
object returned by makeCluster from package parallel, multiple files will be processed in parallel
using clusterMap from package parallel.
Value
A matrix with summary statistics on the processed sequences, containing:
• One column per input file (or pair of input files for paired-end experiments).
• The number of sequences or sequence pairs in rows:
totalSequences - the total number in the in

SystemRequirements GNU make
Description This package provides a framework for the quantification
and analysis of Short Reads. It covers a complete workflow
starting from raw sequence reads, over creation of alignments
and quality control plots, to the quantification of genomic
regions of interest. Read alignments are either generated through
Rbowtie (data from DNA/ChIP/ATAC/Bis-seq experiments) or
Rhisat2 (data from RNA-seq experiments that require spliced alignments),
or can be provided in the form of bam files.

qAlign Align reads
Description
Create read alignments against reference genome and optional auxiliary targets if not yet existing.
If necessary, also build target indices for the aligner.
Usage
qAlign(
sampleFile,
genome,
auxiliaryFile = NULL,
aligner = "Rbowtie",
maxHits = 1,
paired = NULL,
splicedAlignment = FALSE,
snpFile = NULL,
bisulfite = "no",
alignmentParameter = NULL,
projectName = "qProject",
alignmentsDir = NULL,
lib.loc = NULL,
cacheDir = NULL,
clObj = NULL,
checkOnly = FALSE,
geneAnnotation = NULL
)
Arguments
sampleFile The name of a text file listing input sequence files and sample names (see ‘De-
tails’).
genome The reference genome for primary alignments, one of:
• a string referring to a “BSgenome” package (e.g. “"BSgenome.Hsapiens.UCSC.hg19"”),
which will be downloaded automatically from Bioconductor if not present
• the name of a fasta sequence file containing one or several sequences (chro-
mosomes) to be used as a reference. The aligner index will be created when
necessary and stored in a default location (see ‘Details’).
auxiliaryFile The name of a text file listing sequences to be used as additional targets for
alignment of reads not mapping to the reference genome (see ‘Details’).
qAlign 9
aligner selects the aligner program to be used for aligning the reads. Currently, only
“Rbowtie” and “Rhisat2” are supported, which are R wrapper packages for
‘bowtie’ / ‘SpliceMap’ and ‘hisat2’, respectively (see Rbowtie-package and
Rhisat2-package packages).
maxHits sets the maximal number of allowed mapping positions per read (default: 1). If
a read produces more than maxHits alignments, no alignments will be reported
for it. In case of a multi-mapping read, a single alignment is randomly selected.
paired defines the type of paired-end library and can be set to one of no (single read
experiment, default), fr (fw/rev), ff (fw/fw) or rf (rev/fw).
splicedAlignment
If TRUE, reads will be aligned using a spliced aligner, depending on the value of
aligner described above:
aligner="Rhisat2" : This is the recommended setting for spliced alignments
and will use hisat2 from the Rhisat2-package. See also the geneAnnotation
argument below for providing known exon-exon junctions.
aligner="Rbowtie" : This is not recommended and only available for legacy
reasons. It will use SpliceMap to produce spliced alignments (without us-
ing a database of known exon-exon junctions). Compared to the alternative
alignment modes (non-spliced or spliced using Rhisat2 as aligner), this
alignment mode is about ten-fold slower and also less sensitive. Further-
more, SpliceMap can only be used for reads with a minimal length of 50nt;
SpliceMap ignores reads that are shorter, and these reads will not be con-
tained in the BAM file, neither as mapped or unmapped reads.
snpFile The name of a text file listing single nucleotide polymorphisms to be used for
allele-specific alignment and quantification (see ‘Details’).
bisulfite For bisulfite-converted samples (Bis-seq), the type of bisulfite library (“dir” for
directional libraries, “undir” for undirectional libraries).
alignmentParameter
An optional string containing command line parameters to be used for the aligner,
to overrule the default alignment parameters used by QuasR. Please use with
caution; some alignment parameters may break assumptions made by QuasR.
Default parameters are listed in ‘Details’.
projectName An optional name for the alignment project.
alignmentsDir The directory to be used for storing alignments (bam files). If set to NULL (de-
fault), bam files will be generated at the location of the input sequence files.
lib.loc can be used to change the default library path of R. The library path is used
by QuasR to store aligner index packages created from BSgenome reference
genomes.
cacheDir specifies the location to store (potentially huge) temporary files. If set to NULL
(default), the temporary directory of the current R session as returned by tempdir()
will be used.
clObj A cluster object, created by the package parallel, to enable parallel processing
and speed up the alignment process.
10 qAlign
checkOnly If TRUE, prevents the automatic creation of alignments or aligner indices. This
allows to quickly check for missing alignment files without starting the poten-
tially long process of their creation. In the case of missing alignments or indices,
an exception is thrown.
geneAnnotation Only used if aligner is "Rhisat2". The path to either a gtf file or a sqlite
database generated by exporting a TxDb object. This file is used to generate a
splice site file for Rhisat2, that will be used to guide the spliced alignment.
Please note that if using an sqlite database file, do not use the one contained
in the installed package folder of a TxDb package. QuasR (through Rhisat2)
creates additional files in the folder which would interfere with the loading of
the TxDb package.
Details
Before generating new alignments, qAlign looks for previously generated alignments as well as
for an aligner index. If no aligner index exists, it will be automatically created and stored in the
same directory as the provided fasta file, or as an R package in the case of a BSgenome reference.
The name of this R package will be the same as the BSgenome package name, with an additional
suffix from the aligner (e.g. BSgenome.Hsapiens.UCSC.hg19.Rbowtie). The generated bam files
contain both aligned und unaligned reads. For paired-end samples, by default no alignments will be
reported for read pairs where only one of the reads could be aligned.
sampleFile is a tab-delimited text file listing all the input sequences to be included in a given
analysis. The file has either two (single-end) or three columns (paired-end). The first row contains
the column names, and additional rows contain relative or absolute path and name of input sequence
file(s), as well as the according sample name. Three input file formats are supported (fastq, fasta
and bam). All input files in one sampleFile need to be in the same format, and are recognized by
their extension (.fq, .fastq, .fa, .fasta, .fna, .bam), in raw or compressed form (e.g. .fastq.gz). If bam
files are provided, then no alignments are generated by qAlign, and the alignments contained in the
bam files will be used instead.
The column names in sampleFile have to match to the ones in the examples below, for a single-
read experiment:
FileName SampleName
chip_1_1.fq.bz2 Sample1
chip_2_1.fq.bz2 Sample2
and for a paired-end experiment:
FileName1 FileName2 SampleName
rna_1_1.fq.bz2 rna_1_2.fq.bz2 Sample1
rna_2_1.fq.bz2 rna_2_2.fq.bz2 Sample2
The “SampleName” column is the human-readable name for each sample that will be used as sample
labels. Multiple sequence files may be associated to the same sample name, which instructs QuasR
to combine those files.
auxiliaryFile is a tab-delimited text file listing one or several additional target sequence files in
fasta format. Reads that do not map against the reference genome will be

PHRASE Default parameters
idx 14667
Default parameters are listed in ‘Details’.
projectName An optional name for the alignment project.
alignmentsDir The directory to be used for storing alignments (bam files). If set to NULL (de-
fault), bam files will be generated at the location of the input sequence files.
lib.loc can be used to change the default library path of R. The library path is used
by QuasR to store aligner index packages created from BSgenome reference
genomes.
cacheDir specifies the location to store (potentially huge) temporary files. If set to NULL
(default), the temporary directory of the current R session as returned by tempdir()
will be used.
clObj A cluster object, created by the package parallel, to enable parallel processing
and speed up the alignment process.
10 qAlign
checkOnly If TRUE, prevents the automatic creation of alignments or aligner indices. This
allows to quickly check for missing alignment files without starting the poten-
tially long process of their creation. In the case of missing alignments or indices,
an exception is thrown.
geneAnnotation Only used if aligner is "Rhisat2". The path to either a gtf file or a sqlite
database generated by exporting a TxDb object. This file is used to generate a
splice site file for Rhisat2, that will be used to guide the spliced alignment.
Please note that if using an sqlite database file, do not use the one contained
in the installed package folder of a TxDb package. QuasR (through Rhisat2)
creates additional files in the folder which would interfere with the loading of
the TxDb package.
Details
Before generating new alignments, qAlign looks for previously generated alignments as well as
for an aligner index. If no aligner index exists, it will be automatically created and stored in the
same directory as the provided fasta file, or as an R package in the case of a BSgenome reference.
The name of this R package will be the same as the BSgenome package name, with an additional
suffix from the aligner (e.g. BSgenome.Hsapiens.UCSC.hg19.Rbowtie). The generated bam files
contain both aligned und unaligned reads. For paired-end samples, by default no alignments will be
reported for read pairs where only one of the reads could be aligned.

Description This package provides a framework for the quantification
and analysis of Short Reads. It covers a complete workflow
starting from raw sequence reads, over creation of alignments
and quality control plots, to the quantification of genomic
regions of interest. Read alignments are either generated through
Rbowtie (data from DNA/ChIP/ATAC/Bis-seq experiments) or
Rhisat2 (data from RNA-seq experiments that require spliced alignments),
or can be provided in the form of bam files.

filenameMate for paired-end experiments, the name(s) of the input sequence file(s) containing

6 preprocessReads
truncateStartBases
integer(1): the number of bases to be truncated (removed) from the beginning
of each sequence.
truncateEndBases
integer(1): the number of bases to be truncated (removed) from the end of each
sequence.
Lpattern character(1): the left (5’-end) adapter sequence.
Rpattern character(1): the right (3’-end) adapter sequence.
max.Lmismatch mismatch tolerance when searching for matches of Lpattern (see ‘Details’).
max.Rmismatch mismatch tolerance when searching for matches of Rpattern (see ‘Details’).
with.Lindels if TRUE, indels are allowed in the alignments of the suffixes of Lpattern with
the subject, at its beginning (see ‘Details’).
with.Rindels same as with.Lindels but for alignments of the prefixes of Rpattern with the
subject, at its end (see ‘Details’).
minLength integer(1): the minimal allowed sequence length.
nBases integer(1): the maximal number of Ns allowed per sequence.
complexity NULL (default) or numeric(1): If not NULL, the minimal sequence complexity,
as a fraction of the average complexity in the human genome (~3.9bits). For
example, complexity = 0.5 will filter out sequences that do not have at least
half the complexity of the human genome. See ‘Details’ on how the complexity
is calculated.
nrec integer(1): the number of sequence records to read at a time.
clObj a cluster object to be used for parallel processing of multiple files (see ‘Details’).
Details
Sequence files can be in fasta or fastq format, and can be compressed by either gzip, bzip2 or xz
(extensions .gz, .bz2 or .xz). Multiple files can be processed by a single call to preprocessReads;
in that case all sequence file vectors must have identical lengths.
nrec can be used to limit the memory usage when processing large input files. preprocessReads
iteratively loads chunks of nrec sequences from the input until all data been processed.

PATTERN --best idx 19822
ence genome, resulting in two
separate genomes. All reads will be aligned separately to both of these genomes, and the alignments
will be combined, only retaining the best alignment for each read. In the final alignment, each read
will be marked with a tag that classifies it into reference (R), alternative (A) or unknown (U), if the
reads maps equally well to both genomes.
If bisulfite is set to “dir” or “undir”, reads will be C-to-T converted and aligned to a similarly
converted genome.
If alignmentParameter is NULL (recommended), qAlign will select default parameters that are
suitable for the experiment type. Please note that for bisulfite or allele-specific experiments, each
read is aligned multiple times, and resulting alignments need to be combined. This requires special
settings for the alignment parameters that are not recommended to be changed. For ‘simple’ experi-
ments (neither bisulfite, allele-specific, nor spliced), alignments are generated using the parameters
-m maxHits --best --strata. This will align reads with up to “maxHits” best hits in the genome
and selects one of them randomly.
Value
A qProject object.
Author(s)
Anita Lerch, Dimos Gaidatzis, Charlotte Soneson and Michael Stadler

snpFile is a tab-delimited text file without a header and contains four columns with chromosome
name, position, reference allele and alternative allele, as in the example below:
chr1 8596 G A
chr1 18443 G A
chr1 18981 C T
chr1 19341 G A
The reference and alternative alleles will be injected into the reference genome, resulting in two
separate genomes. All reads will be aligned separately to both of these genomes, and the alignments
will be combined, only retaining the best alignment for each read. In the final alignment, each read
will be marked with a tag that classifies it into reference (R), alternative (A) or unknown (U), if the
reads maps equally well to both genomes.

converted genome.

qAlign Align reads
Description
Create read alignments against reference genome and optional auxiliary targets if not yet existing.
If necessary, also build target indices for the aligner.
Usage
qAlign(
sampleFile,
genome,
auxiliaryFile = NULL,
aligner = "Rbowtie",
maxHits = 1,
paired = NULL,
splicedAlignment = FALSE,
snpFile = NULL,
bisulfite = "no",
alignmentParameter = NULL,
projectName = "qProject",
alignmentsDir = NULL,
lib.loc = NULL,
cacheDir = NULL,
clObj = NULL,
checkOnly = FALSE,
geneAnnotation = NULL
)
Arguments
sampleFile The name of a text file listing input sequence files and sample names (see ‘De-
tails’).
genome The reference genome for primary alignments, one of:
• a string referring to a “BSgenome” package (e.g. “"BSgenome.Hsapiens.UCSC.hg19"”),
which will be downloaded automatically from Bioconductor if not present
• the name of a fasta sequence file containing one or several sequences (chro-
mosomes) to be used as a reference. The aligner index will be created when
necessary and stored in a default location (see ‘Details’).
auxiliaryFile The name of a text file listing sequences to be used as additional targets for
alignment of reads not mapping to the reference genome (see ‘Details’).
qAlign 9
aligner selects the aligner program to be used for aligning the reads. Currently, only
“Rbowtie” and “Rhisat2” are supported, which are R wrapper packages for
‘bowtie’ / ‘SpliceMap’ and ‘hisat2’, respectively (see Rbowtie-package and
Rhisat2-package packages).
maxHits sets the maximal number of allowed mapping positions per read (default: 1). If
a read produces more than maxHits alignments, no alignments will be reported
for it. In case of a multi-mapping read, a single alignment is randomly selected.
paired defines the type of paired-end library and can be set to one of no (single read
experiment, default), fr (fw/rev), ff (fw/fw) or rf (rev/fw).
splicedAlignment
If TRUE, reads will be aligned using a spliced aligner, depending on the value of
aligner described above:
aligner="Rhisat2" : This is the recommended setting for spliced alignments
and will use hisat2 from the Rhisat2-package. See also the geneAnnotation
argument below for providing known exon-exon junctions.
aligner="Rbowtie" : This is not recommended and

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Count post-QC TempO-Seq reads against the BioSpyder probe manifest.
     3	
     4	Default analysis choices:
     5	- Bowtie 1.3.1 is used as a command-line equivalent of QuasR's qAlign defaults for
     6	  simple (non-spliced) experiments: `-m 1 --best --strata` with the aligner's
     7	  built-in default seed/mismatch settings.
     8	- Reads are counted only when they produce a unique reported alignment; reads with
     9	  >1 reportable alignments are suppressed by `-m 1`, matching the QuasR/Bowtie
    10	  convention used for this benchmark.
    11	- Count outputs are sparse per-sample TSV files (probe name, raw count); the final
    12	  count matrix is assembled separately in the analysis step.
    13	
    14	This script writes intermediate files under ./work/counts and does not modify ./data.
    15	"""
    16	from __future__ import annotations
    17	
    18	import argparse
    19	import csv
    20	import os
    21	import re
    22	import shlex
    23	import subprocess
    24	from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed

    54	]
    55	
    56	TOTAL_THREADS_DEFAULT = 8
    57	
    58	
    59	def load_sample_table() -> pd.DataFrame:
    60	    sm = pd.read_csv(SAMPLE_MD, sep='\t')
    61	    if set(SAMPLE_ORDER) != set(sm['library_id']):
    62	        missing = sorted(set(SAMPLE_ORDER) - set(sm['library_id']))
    63	        extra = sorted(set(sm['library_id']) - set(SAMPLE_ORDER))
    64	        raise ValueError(f'Sample ID mismatch. missing={missing} extra={extra}')
    65	    sm = sm.set_index('library_id').loc[SAMPLE_ORDER].reset_index()
    66	    return sm
    67	
    68	
    69	def build_reference(outdir: Path) -> Tuple[Path, Path]:
    70	    outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    71	    fasta = outdir / 'temposeq_manifest_probes.fa'
    72	    index_prefix = outdir / 'temposeq_manifest_probes_index'
    73	    if not fasta.exists():
    74	        manifest = pd.read_csv(MANIFEST)
    75	        with fasta.open('w') as handle:
    76	            for _, row in manifest.iterrows():
    77	                handle.write(f'>{row["PROBE_NAME"]}\n{row["PROBE_SEQUENCE"]}\n')
    78	    # Bowtie writes several index files with this prefix. Build only once if the
    79	    # .1.ebwt file is absent.
    80	    if not (index_prefix.with_suffix('.1.ebwt').exists() or Path(str(index_prefix) + '.1.ebwt').exists()):
    81	        cmd = ['bowtie-build', str(fasta), str(index_prefix)]
    82	        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True)
    83	    return fasta, index_prefix
    84	
    85	
    86	def parse_bowtie_log(log_path: Path) -> Dict[str, float]:
    87	    text = log_path.read_text() if log_path.exists() else ''
    88	    stats = {
    89	        'total_reads': float('nan'),
    90	        'reads_with_alignment': float('nan'),
    91	        'reads_failed_to_align': float('nan'),
    92	        'reads_suppressed_due_to_m': float('nan'),
    93	        'reported_alignments': float('nan'),
    94	        'mapping_fraction': float('nan'),
    95	    }
    96	    patterns = {
    97	        'total_reads': r'# reads processed:\s*([0-9,]+)',
    98	        'reads_with_alignment': r'# reads with at least one alignment:\s*([0-9,]+)\s*\(([0-9.]+)%\)',
    99	        'reads_failed_to_align': r'# reads that failed to align:\s*([0-9,]+)\s*\(([0-9.]+)%\)',
   100	        'reads_suppressed_due_to_m': r'# reads with alignments suppressed due to -m:\s*([0-9,]+)\s*\(([0-9.]+)%\)',
   101	        'reported_alignments': r'Reported\s*([0-9,]+)\s*alignments',
   102	    }
   103	    for key, pat in patterns.items():
   104	        m = re.search(pat, text)
   105	        if m:
   106	            stats[key] = float(m.group(1).replace(',', ''))
   107	    if not pd.isna(stats['total_reads']) and not pd.isna(stats['reads_with_alignment']):
   108	        stats['mapping_fraction'] = stats['reads_with_alignment'] / stats['total_reads']
   109	    return stats
   110	
   111	
   112	def count_one_sample(sample_id: str, fastq_path: Path, index_prefix: Path, outdir: Path, threads: int = 1) -> Tuple[str, Path, Path, Dict[str, float]]:
   113	    sample_outdir = outdir / 'by_sample'
   114	    sample_outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   115	    counts_path = sample_outdir / f'{sample_id}.counts.tsv'
   116	    log_path = sample_outdir / f'{sample_id}.bowtie.log'
   117	    if counts_path.exists() and log_path.exists():
   118	        return sample_id, counts_path, log_path, parse_bowtie_log(log_path)
   119	
   120	    # Use a shell pipeline so that bowtie can read streamed FASTQ from pigz.
   121	    cmd = (
   122	        "set -euo pipefail; "
   123	        f"pigz -p 1 -dc {shlex.quote(str(fastq_path))} | "
   124	        f"bowtie -q -S --best --strata -m 1 -p {threads} -x {shlex.quote(str(index_prefix))} - | "
   125	        "awk 'BEGIN{FS="\t"; OFS="\t"} $3 != "*" {c[$3]++} END {for (k in c) print k, c[k]}' | "
   126	        "LC_ALL=C sort -k1,1 > {out}"
   127	    ).format(out=shlex.quote(str(counts_path)))
   128	    with log_path.open('w') as log_handle:
   129	        subprocess.run(['bash', '-lc', cmd], check=True, stdout=log_handle, stderr=log_handle)
   130	    stats = parse_bowtie_log(log_path)
   131	    return sample_id, counts_path, log_path, stats
   132	
   133	
   134	def main() -> None:
   135	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
   136	    parser.add_argument('--workers', type=int, default=4, help='Number of samples to process concurrently.')
   137	    parser.add_argument('--threads-per-bowtie', type=int, default=1, help='Bowtie threads per sample.')
   138	    parser.add_argument('--outdir', type=Path, default=WORK_DIR / 'counts', help='Output directory for sparse counts and logs.')
   139	    args = parser.parse_args()
   140	
   141	    sm = load_sample_table()
   142	    _, index_prefix = build_reference(WORK_DIR / 'temposeq_ref')
   143	
   144	    jobs = []
   145	    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as ex:
   146	        for sample_id in SAMPLE_ORDER:
   147	            fastq = FASTQ_DIR / f'SRR{int(sm.loc[sm.library_id == sample_id, "geo_accession"].index[0])}'
   148	            # The above is not valid mapping; we overwrite with the ENA mapping below.
   149	        
   150	    # Build a robust sample->fastq map from the ENA metadata rather than relying on row order.
   151	    run_md = pd.read_csv(DATA_DIR / 'metadata/ena_run_metadata.tsv', sep='\t')
   152	    run_map = run_md.set_index('sample_title')['run_accession'].to_dict()
   153	    sample_to_fastq = {sid: FASTQ_DIR / f"{run_map[sid]}.fastq.gz" for sid in SAMPLE_ORDER}
   154	
   155	    results = []
   156	    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as ex:
   157	        futs = {ex.submit(count_one_sample, sid, sample_to_fastq[sid], index_prefix, args.outdir, args.threads_per_bowtie): sid for sid in SAMPLE_ORDER}
   158	        for fut in as_completed(futs):
   159	            results.append(fut.result())
   160	            sid, counts_path, log_path, stats = fut.result()
   161	            print(f'{sid}\t{counts_path}\t{stats.get("mapping_fraction", float("nan")):.6f}')
   162	
   163	    summary_rows = []
   164	    for sid, counts_path, log_path, stats in sorted(results, key=lambda x: SAMPLE_ORDER.index(x[0])):
   165	        summary_rows.append({
   166	            'library_id': sid,
   167	            'counts_path': str(counts_path),
   168	            'log_path': str(log_path),
   169	            **stats,
   170	        })
   171	    pd.DataFrame(summary_rows).to_csv(args.outdir / 'mapping_stats.tsv', sep='\t', index=False)
   172	
   173	
   174	if __name__ == '__main__':
   175	    main()
   176

   138	    parser.add_argument('--threads-per-bowtie', type=int, default=1, help='Bowtie threads per sample.')

   140	    args = parser.parse_args()
   141	
   142	    # Validate that the staged sample metadata contains exactly the intended cohort.
   143	    load_sample_table()
   144	    _, index_prefix = build_reference(WORK_DIR / 'temposeq_ref')
   145	
   146	    # Build a robust sample->fastq map from the ENA metadata rather than relying on row order.
   147	    run_md = pd.read_csv(DATA_DIR / 'metadata/ena_run_metadata.tsv', sep='\t')
   148	    run_map = run_md.set_index('sample_title')['run_accession'].to_dict()
   149	    sample_to_fastq = {sid: FASTQ_DIR / f"{run_map[sid]}.fastq.gz" for sid in SAMPLE_ORDER}
   150	
   151	    results = []
   152	    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as ex:
   153	        futs = {
   154	            ex.submit(count_one_sample, sid, sample_to_fastq[sid], index_prefix, args.outdir, args.threads_per_bowtie): sid
   155	            for sid in SAMPLE_ORDER
   156	        }
   157	        for fut in as_completed(futs):
   158	            sid, counts_path, log_path, stats = fut.result()
   159	            results.append((sid, counts_path, log_path, stats))
   160	            print(f'{sid}\t{counts_path}\t{stats.get("mapping_fraction", float("nan")):.6f}')
   161	
   162	    summary_rows = []
   163	    for sid, counts_path, log_path, stats in sorted(results, key=lambda x: SAMPLE_ORDER.index(x[0])):
   164	        summary_rows.append({
   165	            'library_id': sid,
   166	            'counts_path': str(counts_path),
   167	            'log_path': str(log_path),
   168	            **stats,
   169	        })
   170	    args.outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   171	    pd.DataFrame(summary_rows).to_csv(args.outdir / 'mapping_stats.tsv', sep='\t', index=False)
   172	
   173	
   174	if __name__ == '__main__':
   175	    main()
   176	
Review the changes and make sure they are as expected. Edit the file again if necessary.

sm_tp$library_id, drop = FALSE]\n  coldata <- data.frame(\n    row.names = sm_tp$library_id,\n    treatment_ppm = factor(sm_tp$treatment_ppm, levels = all_conc_levels),\n    timepoint = bg = set(background)\n    sig = set(sig_genes) & bg\n    N = len(bg)\n    n = len(sig)\n    rows = []\n    for name, genes in gene_sets.items():\n        gs = set(genes) & bg\n        K = len(gs)\n        if K == 0:\n            continue\n        k = len(sig & gs)\n        if n == 0 or k == 0:\n            p = 1.0\n        else:\n            p = float(hypergeom.sf(k - 1, N, K, n))\n        rows.append((name, p, k, K, N, n))\n    if not rows:\n        return pd.DataFrame(columns=['gene_set', 'p_value', 'fdr', 'significant_in_gene_set'])\n    pvals = [r[1] for r in rows]\n    fdr = no_plot=True,\n        threads=8,\n        verbose=False,\n    )\n    res = pre.res2d.copy()\n    if res.empty:\n        return pd.DataFrame(columns=['gene_set', 'stat_dist_dir', 'p_value', 'fdr'])\n    # Standardize column names.\n    rename_map = {}\n    for col in res.columns:\n        if col.lower() == 'term':\n            rename_map[col] = 'gene_set'\n        elif col.lower() == 'nes':\n            rename_map[col] = 'stat_dist_dir'\n        elif 'nom' in col.lower() and 'p' in col.lower():\n            rename_map[col] = 'p_value'\n        elif 'fdr' in col.lower():\n     82	def collapse_probe_stats_for_gsea(df: pd.DataFrame, probe_map: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    83	    """Collapse probe-level statistics to one representative probe per human gene.
    84	
    85	    The representative probe is selected by largest absolute Wald statistic, then
    86	    smallest padj, then smallest probe ID.
    87	    """
    88	    merged = df.merge(probe_map[['gene_probe_id', 'human_symbol']], on='gene_probe_id', how='left')
    89	    merged = merged[merged['human_symbol'].notna() & (merged['human_symbol'] != '')].copy()
    90	    merged['abs_stat'] = merged['stat'].abs()
    91	    merged = merged.sort_values(['human_symbol', 'abs_stat', 'padj', 'gene_probe_id'], ascending=[True, False, True, True])
    92	    rep = merged.drop_duplicates('human_symbol', keep='first').copy()
    93	    rep = rep[['human_symbol', 'stat', 'log2FoldChange', 'padj', 'base_mean', 'gene_probe_id']]

    97	    rep = rep.sort_values('stat', ascending=False)
    98	    return rep

   101	def significant_gene_sets(df: pd.DataFrame, probe_map: pd.DataFrame) -> Tuple[set, set, set]:
   102	    """Return unique human-gene sets for non-directional, up, and down significant genes.
   103	
   104	    The representative significant probe per gene is the significant probe with the
   105	    largest absolute Wald statistic.
   106	    """
   107	    merged = df.merge(probe_map[['gene_probe_id', 'human_symbol']], on='gene_probe_id', how='left')
   108	    merged = merged[merged['human_symbol'].notna() & (merged['human_symbol'] != '')].copy()
   109	    merged = merged[merged['padj'].notna() & (merged['padj'] < 0.10)].copy()
   110	    if merged.empty:
   111	        return set(), set(), set()
   112	    merged['abs_stat'] = merged['stat'].abs()
   113	    merged = merged.sort_values(['human_symbol', 'abs_stat', 'padj', 'gene_probe_id'], ascending=[True, False, True, True])
   114	    rep = merged.drop_duplicates('human_symbol', keep='first').copy()
   115	    sig_all = set(rep['human_symbol'])
   116	    sig_up = set(rep.loc[rep['log2FoldChange'] > 0, 'human_symbol'])
   117	    sig_down = set(rep.loc[rep['log2FoldChange'] < 0, 'human_symbol'])
   118	    return sig_all, sig_up, sig_down

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Count post-QC TempO-Seq reads against the BioSpyder probe manifest.

Default analysis choices:
- Bowtie 1.3.1 is used as a command-line equivalent of QuasR's qAlign defaults for
  simple (non-spliced) experiments: `-m 1 --best --strata` with the aligner's
  built-in default seed/mismatch settings.
- Reads are counted only when they produce a unique reported alignment; reads with
  >1 reportable alignments are suppressed by `-m 1`, matching the QuasR/Bowtie
  convention used for this benchmark.
- Count outputs are sparse per-sample TSV files (probe name, raw count); the final
  count matrix is assembled separately in the analysis step.

This script writes intermediate files under ./work/counts and does not modify ./data.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import os
import re
import shlex
import subprocess
from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple

import pandas as pd

DATA_DIR = Path('/data')
WORK_DIR = Path('/workspace/work')
MANIFEST = DATA_DIR / 'metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv'
FASTQ_DIR = DATA_DIR / 'raw/GSE154899/fastq'
SAMPLE_MD = DATA_DIR / 'metadata/sample_metadata.tsv'

SAMPLE_ORDER = [
    '18A0839','18A0840','18A0841','18A0842','18A0843',
    '18A0849','18A0850','18A0851','18A0852','18A0853',
    '18A0869','18A0870','18A0871','18A0872','18A0873',
    '18A0879','18A0880','18A0881','18A0882','18A0883',
    '18A0899','18A0901','18A0903','18A0909','18A0910','18A0912','18A0913',
    '18A0929','18A0930','18A0932','18A0933',
    '18A0939','18A0940','18A0941','18A0942','18A0943',
    '18A0959','18A0960','18A0961','18A0962','18A0963',
    '18A0969','18A0970','18A0971','18A0972','18A0973',
    '18A0989','18A0990','18A0991','18A0992','18A0993',
    '18A0999','18A1000','18A1001','18A1002','18A1003',
    '18A0829','18A0830','18A0831','18A0832','18A0833',
    '18A0859','18A0860','18A0861','18A0862','18A0863',
    '18A0889','18A0890','18A0891','18A0893',
    '18A0919','18A0920','18A0921','18A0922','18A0923',
    '18A0949','18A0950','18A0951','18A0952','18A0953',
    '18A0979','18A0980','18A0981','18A0982','18A0983'
]

TOTAL_THREADS_DEFAULT = 8


def load_sample_table() -> pd.DataFrame:
    sm = pd.read_csv(SAMPLE_MD, sep='\t')
    if set(SAMPLE_ORDER) != set(sm['library_id']):
        missing = sorted(set(SAMPLE_ORDER) - set(sm['library_id']))
        extra = sorted(set(sm['library_id']) - set(SAMPLE_ORDER))
        raise ValueError(f'Sample ID mismatch. missing={missing} extra={extra}')
    sm = sm.set_index('library_id').loc[SAMPLE_ORDER].reset_index()
    return sm


def build_reference(outdir: Path) -> Tuple[Path, Path]:
    outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    fasta = outdir / 'temposeq_manifest_probes.fa'
    index_prefix = outdir / 'temposeq_manifest_probes_index'
    if not fasta.exists():
        manifest = pd.read_csv(MANIFEST)
        with fasta.open('w') as handle:
            for _, row in manifest.iterrows():
                handle.write(f'>{row["PROBE_NAME"]}\n{row["PROBE_SEQUENCE"]}\n')
    # Bowtie writes several index files with this prefix. Build only once if the
    # .1.ebwt file is absent.
    if not (index_prefix.with_suffix('.1.ebwt').exists() or Path(str(index_prefix) + '.1.ebwt').exists()):
        cmd = ['bowtie-build', str(fasta), str(index_prefix)]
        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True)
    return fasta, index_prefix


def parse_bowtie_log(log_path: Path) -> Dict[str, float]:
    text = log_path.read_text() if log_path.exists() else ''
    stats = {
        'total_reads': float('nan'),
        'reads_with_alignment': float('nan'),
        'reads_failed_to_align': float('nan'),
        'reads_suppressed_due_to_m': float('nan'),
        'reported_alignments': float('nan'),
        'mapping_fraction': float('nan'),
    }
    patterns = {
        'total_reads': r'# reads processed:\s*([0-9,]+)',
        'reads_with_alignment': r'# reads with at least one alignment:\s*([0-9,]+)\s*\(([0-9.]+)%\)',
        'reads_failed_to_align': r'# reads that failed to align:\s*([0-9,]+)\s*\(([0-9.]+)%\)',
        'reads_suppressed_due_to_m': r'# reads with alignments suppressed due to -m:\s*([0-9,]+)\s*\(([0-9.]+)%\)',
        'reported_alignments': r'Reported\s*([0-9,]+)\s*alignments',
    }
    for key, pat in patterns.items():
        m = re.search(pat, text)
        if m:
            stats[key] = float(m.group(1).replace(',', ''))
    if not pd.isna(stats['total_reads']) and not pd.isna(stats['reads_with_alignment']):
2026-11-14T23:27:43.856756+00:00 · bash_session · { "action": "type_submit", "input": "sed -n '221,420p' /workspace/work/count_temposeq.py" }
{
  "action": "type_submit",
  "input": "sed -n '221,420p' /workspace/work/count_temposeq.py"
}

        stats['mapping_fraction'] = stats['reads_with_alignment'] / stats['total_reads']
    return stats


def count_one_sample(sample_id: str, fastq_path: Path, index_prefix: Path, outdir: Path, threads: int = 1) -> Tuple[str, Path, Path, Dict[str, float]]:
    sample_outdir = outdir / 'by_sample'
    sample_outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    counts_path = sample_outdir / f'{sample_id}.counts.tsv'
    log_path = sample_outdir / f'{sample_id}.bowtie.log'
    if counts_path.exists() and log_path.exists():
        return sample_id, counts_path, log_path, parse_bowtie_log(log_path)

    # Use a shell pipeline so that bowtie can read streamed FASTQ from pigz.
    awk_script = r'BEGIN{FS="\t"; OFS="\t"} $3 != "*" {c[$3]++} END {for (k in c) print k, c[k]}'
    cmd = (
        'set -euo pipefail; '
        f"pigz -p 1 -dc {shlex.quote(str(fastq_path))} | "
        f"bowtie -q -S --best --strata -m 1 -p {threads} -x {shlex.quote(str(index_prefix))} - | "
        f"awk '{awk_script}' | "
        f"LC_ALL=C sort -k1,1 > {shlex.quote(str(counts_path))}"
    )
    with log_path.open('w') as log_handle:
        subprocess.run(['bash', '-lc', cmd], check=True, stdout=log_handle, stderr=log_handle)
    stats = parse_bowtie_log(log_path)
    return sample_id, counts_path, log_path, stats


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--workers', type=int, default=4, help='Number of samples to process concurrently.')
    parser.add_argument('--threads-per-bowtie', type=int, default=1, help='Bowtie threads per sample.')
    parser.add_argument('--outdir', type=Path, default=WORK_DIR / 'counts', help='Output directory for sparse counts and logs.')
    args = parser.parse_args()

    # Validate that the staged sample metadata contains exactly the intended cohort.
    load_sample_table()
    _, index_prefix = build_reference(WORK_DIR / 'temposeq_ref')

    # Build a robust sample->fastq map from the ENA metadata rather than relying on row order.
    run_md = pd.read_csv(DATA_DIR / 'metadata/ena_run_metadata.tsv', sep='\t')
    run_map = run_md.set_index('sample_title')['run_accession'].to_dict()
    sample_to_fastq = {sid: FASTQ_DIR / f"{run_map[sid]}.fastq.gz" for sid in SAMPLE_ORDER}

    results = []
    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as ex:
        futs = {
            ex.submit(count_one_sample, sid, sample_to_fastq[sid], index_prefix, args.outdir, args.threads_per_bowtie): sid
            for sid in SAMPLE_ORDER
        }
        for fut in as_completed(futs):
            sid, counts_path, log_path, stats = fut.result()
            results.append((sid, counts_path, log_path, stats))
            print(f'{sid}\t{counts_path}\t{stats.get("mapping_fraction", float("nan")):.6f}')

    summary_rows = []
    for sid, counts_path, log_path, stats in sorted(results, key=lambda x: SAMPLE_ORDER.index(x[0])):
        summary_rows.append({
            'library_id': sid,
            'counts_path': str(counts_path),
            'log_path': str(log_path),
            **stats,
        })
    args.outdir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    pd.DataFrame(summary_rows).to_csv(args.outdir / 'mapping_stats.tsv', sep='\t', index=False)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Assemble the sparse per-sample TempO-Seq probe counts into the final matrix.

This script expects one sparse TSV per sample under work/counts/by_sample with two
columns: probe name and raw count. It writes the dense count matrix required by
outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

DATA_DIR = Path('/data')
WORK_DIR = Path('/workspace/work')
MANIFEST = DATA_DIR / 'metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv'
SAMPLE_ORDER = [
    '18A0839','18A0840','18A0841','18A0842','18A0843',
    '18A0849','18A0850','18A0851','18A0852','18A0853',
    '18A0869','18A0870','18A0871','18A0872','18A0873',
    '18A0879','18A0880','18A0881','18A0882','18A0883',
    '18A0899','18A0901','18A0903','18A0909','18A0910','18A0912','18A0913',
    '18A0929','18A0930','18A0932','18A0933',
    '18A0939','18A0940','18A0941','18A0942','18A0943',
    '18A0959','18A0960','18A0961','18A0962','18A0963',
    '18A0969','18A0970','18A0971','18A0972','18A0973',
    '18A0989','18A0990','18A0991','18A0992','18A0993',
    '18A0999','18A1000','18A1001','18A1002','18A1003',
    '18A0829','18A0830','18A0831','18A0832','18A0833',
    '18A0859','18A0860','18A0861','18A0862','18A0863',
    '18A0889','18A0890','18A0891','18A0893',
    '18A0919','18A0920','18A0921','18A0922','18A0923',
    '18A0949','18A0950','18A0951','18A0952','18A0953',
    '18A0979','18A0980','18A0981','18A0982','18A0983'
]


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--counts-dir', type=Path, default=WORK_DIR / 'counts' / 'by_sample')
    parser.add_argument('--out', type=Path, default=Path('/workspace/outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv'))
    args = parser.parse_args()

    manifest = pd.read_csv(MANIFEST, usecols=['PROBE_NAME'])
    probe_names = manifest['PROBE_NAME'].tolist()
    matrix = pd.DataFrame(0, index=probe_names, columns=SAMPLE_ORDER, dtype=np.int32)

    for sample in SAMPLE_ORDER:
        path = args.counts_dir / f'{sample}.counts.tsv'
        if not path.exists():
            raise FileNotFoundError(f'Missing counts file: {path}')
        df = pd.read_csv(path, sep='\t', header=None, names=['gene_probe_id', 'count'])
        if df.empty:
            continue
        counts = df.set_index('gene_probe_id')['count'].astype(np.int32)
        missing = counts.index.difference(matrix.index)
        if len(missing) > 0:
            raise ValueError(f'Counts file for {sample} contains unknown probes: {missing[:5].tolist()}')
        matrix.loc[counts.index, sample] = counts.values

    args.out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    matrix.insert(0, 'gene_probe_id', matrix.index)
    matrix.to_csv(args.out, index=False)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

2026-11-14T23:28:55.657578+00:00 · bash_session · { "action": "type_submit", "input": "sed -n '1,260p' /workspace/work/deseq2_contrasts.R" }

#!/usr/bin/env Rscript
# Differential expression analysis for TempO-Seq probe counts.
#
# Analysis choices:
# - DESeq2 is fit separately within each timepoint (day7/day28/day90), using a
#   treatment factor with 0ppm as the reference level.
# - Wald tests compare each nonzero concentration to the timepoint-matched 0ppm
#   control.
# - Results are filtered at BH FDR < 0.10.
# - The output remains at probe level (PROBE_NAME row key).
#
# Inputs:
#   /workspace/outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv
#   /data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv
# Outputs:
#   /workspace/outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv

suppressPackageStartupMessages({
  library(data.table)
  library(DESeq2)
  library(dplyr)
})

sample_order <- c(
  '18A0839','18A0840','18A0841','18A0842','18A0843',
  '18A0849','18A0850','18A0851','18A0852','18A0853',
  '18A0869','18A0870','18A0871','18A0872','18A0873',
  '18A0879','18A0880','18A0881','18A0882','18A0883',
  '18A0899','18A0901','18A0903','18A0909','18A0910','18A0912','18A0913',
  '18A0929','18A0930','18A0932','18A0933',
  '18A0939','18A0940','18A0941','18A0942','18A0943',
  '18A0959','18A0960','18A0961','18A0962','18A0963',
  '18A0969','18A0970','18A0971','18A0972','18A0973',
  '18A0989','18A0990','18A0991','18A0992','18A0993',
  '18A0999','18A1000','18A1001','18A1002','18A1003',
  '18A0829','18A0830','18A0831','18A0832','18A0833',
  '18A0859','18A0860','18A0861','18A0862','18A0863',
  '18A0889','18A0890','18A0891','18A0893',
  '18A0919','18A0920','18A0921','18A0922','18A0923',
  '18A0949','18A0950','18A0951','18A0952','18A0953',
  '18A0979','18A0980','18A0981','18A0982','18A0983'
)

counts_path <- '/workspace/outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv'
out_path <- '/workspace/outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv'
full_out_path <- '/workspace/work/deseq2_all_results.csv'

counts_dt <- fread(counts_path)
stopifnot(names(counts_dt)[1] == 'gene_probe_id')
stopifnot(all(sample_order %in% names(counts_dt)))
counts_mat <- as.matrix(counts_dt[, ..sample_order])
rownames(counts_mat) <- counts_dt$gene_probe_id
storage.mode(counts_mat) <- 'integer'

sample_md <- fread('/data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv')
sample_md <- sample_md[match(sample_order, sample_md$library_id)]
stopifnot(all(sample_md$library_id == sample_order))

all_conc_levels <- c('0ppm', '40ppm', '200ppm', '600ppm', '2000ppm', '6000ppm')

timepoints <- c('day7', 'day28', 'day90')
res_list <- list()
full_list <- list()

for (tp in timepoints) {
  idx <- which(sample_md$timepoint == tp)
  sm_tp <- sample_md[idx]
  mat_tp <- counts_mat[, sm_tp$library_id, drop = FALSE]
  coldata <- data.frame(
    row.names = sm_tp$library_id,
    treatment_ppm = factor(sm_tp$treatment_ppm, levels = all_conc_levels),
    timepoint = factor(sm_tp$timepoint, levels = timepoints)
  )
  dds <- DESeqDataSetFromMatrix(countData = mat_tp, colData = coldata, design = ~ treatment_ppm)
  dds <- DESeq(dds, quiet = TRUE)
  for (conc in setdiff(all_conc_levels, '0ppm')) {
    if (!conc %in% as.character(sm_tp$treatment_ppm)) {
      next
    }
    res <- results(dds, contrast = c('treatment_ppm', conc, '0ppm'), alpha = 0.10)
    res_df <- as.data.frame(res)
    res_df$gene_probe_id <- rownames(res_df)
    res_df$timepoint <- tp
    res_df$concentration <- conc
    res_df$contrast_gene_id <- paste(tp, conc, res_df$gene_probe_id, sep = '|')
    res_df <- res_df[, c('contrast_gene_id', 'timepoint', 'concentration', 'gene_probe_id', 'baseMean', 'log2FoldChange', 'lfcSE', 'stat', 'pvalue', 'padj')]
    colnames(res_df) <- c('contrast_gene_id', 'timepoint', 'concentration', 'gene_probe_id', 'base_mean', 'log2FoldChange', 'lfcSE', 'stat', 'pvalue', 'padj')
    full_list[[paste(tp, conc, sep='|')]] <- res_df
    sig_df <- res_df[!is.na(res_df$padj) & res_df$padj < 0.10, ]
    res_list[[paste(tp, conc, sep='|')]] <- sig_df
  }
}

full_out <- bind_rows(full_list)
write.csv(full_out, full_out_path, row.names = FALSE, quote = FALSE)
out <- bind_rows(res_list)
write.csv(out, out_path, row.names = FALSE, quote = FALSE)

2026-11-14T23:38:29.866427+00:00 · bash_session · { "action": "type_submit", "input": "sed -n '1,320p' /workspace/work/pathway_enrichment.py" }

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Canonical pathway and curated DNA-damage enrichment analyses.

This script consumes the full DESeq2 probe-level results and the probe-to-human
ortholog mapping. It collapses probe-level statistics to one representative human
symbol per gene for preranked GSEA, and it uses significant DESeq2 genes for
hypergeometric overrepresentation analyses.

Because the Bioconductor package piano could not be compiled cleanly in the
available environment, preranked enrichment is implemented with gseapy's
prerank algorithm as a practical substitute. Hypergeometric ORA uses scipy and
BH adjustment from statsmodels.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import math
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, Iterable, List, Sequence, Tuple

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from openpyxl import load_workbook
from scipy.stats import hypergeom
from statsmodels.stats.multitest import multipletests
import gseapy as gp

WORK_DIR = Path('/workspace/work')
OUT_DIR = Path('/workspace/outputs')

DESEQ_ALL = WORK_DIR / 'deseq2_all_results.csv'
PROBE_MAP = WORK_DIR / 'probe_human_map.tsv'
CP_GMT = WORK_DIR / 'msigdb/c2.cp.v6.2.symbols.gmt'
HALLMARK_GMT = WORK_DIR / 'msigdb/h.all.v6.2.symbols.gmt'
DNA_XLSX = Path('/data/metadata/supplementary/mmc3_dna_damage_gene_sets.xlsx')

CANONICAL_GSEA_OUT = OUT_DIR / 'piano_preranked_gsea.csv'
CANONICAL_ORA_OUT = OUT_DIR / 'hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment.csv'
DNA_OUT = OUT_DIR / 'dna_damage_enrichment.csv'

TIMEPOINTS = ['day7', 'day28', 'day90']
ALL_CONCS = ['40ppm', '200ppm', '600ppm', '2000ppm', '6000ppm']
DNA_CONTRASTS = [
    ('day28', c) for c in ALL_CONCS
] + [
    ('day90', c) for c in ALL_CONCS
] + [
    ('day7', c) for c in ['600ppm', '2000ppm', '6000ppm']
]


def read_gmt(path: Path) -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
    gene_sets: Dict[str, List[str]] = {}
    with path.open() as handle:
        for line in handle:
            line = line.rstrip('\n')
            if not line:
                continue
            parts = line.split('\t')
            name = parts[0]
            genes = [g for g in parts[2:] if g]
            gene_sets[name] = sorted(set(genes))
    return gene_sets


def combine_canonical_sets() -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
    gene_sets = {}
    for path in [CP_GMT, HALLMARK_GMT]:
        gene_sets.update(read_gmt(path))
    return gene_sets


def filter_gene_sets_to_universe(gene_sets: Dict[str, List[str]], universe: Sequence[str], min_size: int = 5, max_size: int = 500) -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
    universe = set(universe)
    filtered = {}
    for name, genes in gene_sets.items():
        gs = sorted(set(genes) & universe)
        if min_size <= len(gs) <= max_size:
            filtered[name] = gs
    return filtered


def collapse_probe_stats_for_gsea(df: pd.DataFrame, probe_map: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Collapse probe-level statistics to one representative probe per human gene.

    The representative probe is selected by largest absolute Wald statistic, then
    smallest padj, then smallest probe ID.
    """
    merged = df.merge(probe_map[['gene_probe_id', 'human_symbol']], on='gene_probe_id', how='left')
    merged = merged[merged['human_symbol'].notna() & (merged['human_symbol'] != '')].copy()
    merged['abs_stat'] = merged['stat'].abs()
    merged = merged.sort_values(['human_symbol', 'abs_stat', 'padj', 'gene_probe_id'], ascending=[True, False, True, True])
    rep = merged.drop_duplicates('human_symbol', keep='first').copy()
    rep = rep[['human_symbol', 'stat', 'log2FoldChange', 'padj', 'base_mean', 'gene_probe_id']]
    rep = rep.rename(columns={'human_symbol': 'gene_symbol'})
    rep = rep.dropna(subset=['stat'])
    rep = rep.sort_values('stat', ascending=False)
    return rep


def significant_gene_sets(df: pd.DataFrame, probe_map: pd.DataFrame) -> Tuple[set, set, set]:
    """Return unique human-gene sets for non-directional, up, and down significant genes.

    The representative significant probe per gene is the significant probe with the
    largest absolute Wald statistic.
    """
    merged = df.merge(probe_map[['gene_probe_id', 'human_symbol']], on='gene_probe_id', how='left')
    merged = merged[merged['human_symbol'].notna() & (merged['human_symbol'] != '')].copy()
    merged = merged[merged['padj'].notna() & (merged['padj'] < 0.10)].copy()
    if merged.empty:
        return set(), set(), set()
2026-11-14T23:38:34.057261+00:00 · bash_session · { "action": "type_submit", "input": "sed -n '321,640p' /workspace/work/pathway_enrichment.py" }
{
  "action": "type_submit",
  "input": "sed -n '321,640p' /workspace/work/pathway_enrichment.py"
}

    merged['abs_stat'] = merged['stat'].abs()
    merged = merged.sort_values(['human_symbol', 'abs_stat', 'padj', 'gene_probe_id'], ascending=[True, False, True, True])
    rep = merged.drop_duplicates('human_symbol', keep='first').copy()
    sig_all = set(rep['human_symbol'])
    sig_up = set(rep.loc[rep['log2FoldChange'] > 0, 'human_symbol'])
    sig_down = set(rep.loc[rep['log2FoldChange'] < 0, 'human_symbol'])
    return sig_all, sig_up, sig_down


def hypergeom_enrichment(sig_genes: set, gene_sets: Dict[str, List[str]], background: set) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Run one-sided hypergeometric ORA for a single gene list."""
    bg = set(background)
    sig = set(sig_genes) & bg
    N = len(bg)
    n = len(sig)
    rows = []
    for name, genes in gene_sets.items():
        gs = set(genes) & bg
        K = len(gs)
        if K == 0:
            continue
        k = len(sig & gs)
        if n == 0 or k == 0:
            p = 1.0
        else:
            p = float(hypergeom.sf(k - 1, N, K, n))
        rows.append((name, p, k, K, N, n))
    if not rows:
        return pd.DataFrame(columns=['gene_set', 'p_value', 'fdr', 'significant_in_gene_set'])
    pvals = [r[1] for r in rows]
    fdr = multipletests(pvals, method='fdr_bh')[1]
    out = pd.DataFrame({
        'gene_set': [r[0] for r in rows],
        'p_value': pvals,
        'fdr': fdr,
        'significant_in_gene_set': [r[2] for r in rows],
        'genes_total': [r[3] for r in rows],
        'background_total': [r[4] for r in rows],
        'significant_total': [r[5] for r in rows],
    })
    return out


def run_preranked_gsea(rank_df: pd.DataFrame, gene_sets: Dict[str, List[str]], seed: int = 1) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Run preranked GSEA with gseapy and return the result table.

    rank_df must contain columns gene_symbol and stat sorted in descending order.
    """
    if rank_df.empty:
        return pd.DataFrame(columns=['gene_set', 'stat_dist_dir', 'p_value', 'fdr'])
    rnk = rank_df[['gene_symbol', 'stat']].copy()
    rnk = rnk.dropna()
    rnk = rnk.sort_values('stat', ascending=False)
    # gseapy expects unique gene names.
    rnk = rnk.drop_duplicates('gene_symbol', keep='first')
    if rnk.empty:
        return pd.DataFrame(columns=['gene_set', 'stat_dist_dir', 'p_value', 'fdr'])
    pre = gp.prerank(
        rnk=rnk,
        gene_sets=gene_sets,
        permutation_num=1000,
        seed=seed,
        outdir=None,
        format='png',
        min_size=5,
        max_size=500,
        no_plot=True,
        threads=8,
        verbose=False,
    )
    res = pre.res2d.copy()
    if res.empty:
        return pd.DataFrame(columns=['gene_set', 'stat_dist_dir', 'p_value', 'fdr'])
    # Standardize column names.
    rename_map = {}
    for col in res.columns:
        if col.lower() == 'term':
            rename_map[col] = 'gene_set'
        elif col.lower() == 'nes':
            rename_map[col] = 'stat_dist_dir'
        elif 'nom' in col.lower() and 'p' in col.lower():
            rename_map[col] = 'p_value'
        elif 'fdr' in col.lower():
            rename_map[col] = 'fdr'
    res = res.rename(columns=rename_map)
    if 'gene_set' not in res.columns:
        res['gene_set'] = res.index
    if 'stat_dist_dir' not in res.columns and 'NES' in res.columns:
        res['stat_dist_dir'] = res['NES']
    if 'p_value' not in res.columns and 'pval' in res.columns:
        res['p_value'] = res['pval']
    if 'fdr' not in res.columns and 'FDR q-val' in res.columns:
        res['fdr'] = res['FDR q-val']
    res = res[['gene_set', 'stat_dist_dir', 'p_value', 'fdr']].copy()
    res['stat_dist_dir'] = pd.to_numeric(res['stat_dist_dir'], errors='coerce')
    res['p_value'] = pd.to_numeric(res['p_value'], errors='coerce')
    res['fdr'] = pd.to_numeric(res['fdr'], errors='coerce')
    return res


def gene_counts_in_set(rank_df: pd.DataFrame, genes: Sequence[str]) -> Tuple[int, int, int, float, float]:
    gs = set(genes)
    sub = rank_df[rank_df['gene_symbol'].isin(gs)]
    up = int((sub['stat'] > 0).sum())
    down = int((sub['stat'] < 0).sum())
    total = len(gs)
    return total, up, down, 100.0 * up / total if total else np.nan, 100.0 * down / total if total else np.nan


def load_dna_gene_sets(xlsx_path: Path) -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
    wb = load_workbook(xlsx_path, read_only=True, data_only=True)
    ws = wb[wb.sheetnames[0]]
    gene_sets: Dict[str, List[str]] = {}
    for row in ws.iter_rows(values_only=True):
        name = row[0]
        if not isinstance(name, str):
            continue
        if name in {'Name of Geneset'} or name.startswith('Supplemental Table S4'):
            continue
        genes = [g for g in row[2:] if isinstance(g, str) and g != '']
        gene_sets[name] = sorted(set(genes))
    if 'GO_CELLULAR_RESPONSE_TO_DNA_DAMAGE_STIMULUS' in gene_sets:
        gene_sets.pop('GO_CELLULAR_RESPONSE_TO_DNA_DAMAGE_STIMULUS')
    return gene_sets


def main() -> None:
    OUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    probe_map = pd.read_csv(PROBE_MAP, sep='\t')
    full = pd.read_csv(DESEQ_ALL)
    full = full.rename(columns={'base_mean': 'base_mean'})
    # Ensure numeric types.
    for col in ['base_mean', 'log2FoldChange', 'lfcSE', 'stat', 'pvalue', 'padj']:
        full[col] = pd.to_numeric(full[col], errors='coerce')

    # Canonical pathway collections.
    canonical_sets = combine_canonical_sets()

    gsea_rows = []
    ora_rows = []

    for tp in TIMEPOINTS:
        for conc in ALL_CONCS:
            sub = full[(full['timepoint'] == tp) & (full['concentration'] == conc)].copy()
            if sub.empty:
                continue
            rep = collapse_probe_stats_for_gsea(sub, probe_map)
            universe = set(rep['gene_symbol'])
            filtered_sets = filter_gene_sets_to_universe(canonical_sets, universe, min_size=5, max_size=500)
            # Directional preranked GSEA.
            gsea_res = run_preranked_gsea(rep[['gene_symbol', 'stat']], filtered_sets, seed=1)
            if not gsea_res.empty:
                # Add direction and per-set counts.
                rep_index = rep.set_index('gene_symbol')
                for _, row in gsea_res.iterrows():
                    gs = filtered_sets[row['gene_set']]
                    total, up, down, pct_up, pct_down = gene_counts_in_set(rep, gs)
                    direction = 'up' if row['stat_dist_dir'] >= 0 else 'down'
                    gsea_rows.append({
                        'gsea_result_id': f'{tp}|{conc}|{direction}|{row["gene_set"]}',
                        'timepoint': tp,
                        'concentration': conc,
                        'direction': direction,
                        'gene_set': row['gene_set'],
                        'genes_total': total,
                        'stat_dist_dir': row['stat_dist_dir'],
                        'p_value': row['p_value'],
                        'fdr': row['fdr'],
                        'genes_up': up,
                        'genes_down': down,
                        'percent_up_genes': pct_up,
                        'percent_down_genes': pct_down,
                    })

            # Hypergeometric ORA using significant genes.
            sig_all, sig_up, sig_down = significant_gene_sets(sub, probe_map)
            for direction, sig in [('non_directional', sig_all), ('up', sig_up), ('down', sig_down)]:
                ora = hypergeom_enrichment(sig, filtered_sets, universe)
                if ora.empty:
                    continue
                ora['timepoint'] = tp
                ora['concentration'] = conc
                ora['direction'] = direction
                ora['gene_set'] = ora['gene_set'].astype(str)
                ora['hypergeometric_result_id'] = ora.apply(lambda r: f'{tp}|{conc}|{direction}|{r["gene_set"]}', axis=1)
                ora = ora[['hypergeometric_result_id', 'timepoint', 'concentration', 'direction', 'gene_set', 'p_value', 'fdr', 'significant_in_gene_set']]
                ora_rows.append(ora)

    gsea_out = pd.DataFrame(gsea_rows)
    if not gsea_out.empty:
        gsea_out = gsea_out[gsea_out['fdr'] < 0.10].copy()
        gsea_out = gsea_out.sort_values(['timepoint', 'concentration', 'direction', 'fdr', 'p_value', 'gene_set'])
    gsea_out.to_csv(CANONICAL_GSEA_OUT, index=False)

    ora_out = pd.concat(ora_rows, ignore_index=True) if ora_rows else pd.DataFrame(columns=['hypergeometric_result_id', 'timepoint', 'concentration', 'direction', 'gene_set', 'p_value', 'fdr', 'significant_in_gene_set'])
    if not ora_out.empty:
        ora_out = ora_out[ora_out['fdr'] < 0.10].copy()
        ora_out = ora_out.sort_values(['timepoint', 'concentration', 'direction', 'fdr', 'p_value', 'gene_set'])
    ora_out.to_csv(CANONICAL_ORA_OUT, index=False)

    # Curated DNA-damage enrichment.
    dna_sets = load_dna_gene_sets(DNA_XLSX)
    dna_background = set().union(*dna_sets.values())
    dna_rows = []
    for tp, conc in DNA_CONTRASTS:
        sub = full[(full['timepoint'] == tp) & (full['concentration'] == conc)].copy()
        if sub.empty:
            continue
        sig_all, sig_up, sig_down = significant_gene_sets(sub, probe_map)
        # Background for DNA analyses is the union of the retained curated sets.
root@c7d1f59942ba:/workspace#         for gene_set, genes in dna_sets.items():
            gs = set(genes) & dna_background
            if not gs:
                continue
            for direction, sig in [('non_directional', sig_all), ('up', sig_up), ('down', sig_down)]:
                bg = dna_background
                sig_bg = set(sig) & bg
                N = len(bg)
                n = len(sig_bg)
                K = len(gs)
                k = len(sig_bg & gs)
                p = 1.0 if n == 0 or k == 0 else float(hypergeom.sf(k - 1, N, K, n))
                dna_rows.append({
                    'dna_damage_result_id': f'{tp}|{conc}|{gene_set}',
                    'timepoint': tp,
                    'concentration': conc,
                    'gene_set': gene_set,
                    'direction': direction,
                    'p_value': p,
                    'significant_in_gene_set': k,
                })
    dna_out = pd.DataFrame(dna_rows)
    # Compute FDR within each contrast and direction.
    if not dna_out.empty:
        dna_out['fdr'] = np.nan
        for (tp, conc, direction), idx in dna_out.groupby(['timepoint', 'concentration', 'direction']).groups.items():
            pvals = dna_out.loc[idx, 'p_value'].values
            if len(pvals) == 0:
                continue
            dna_out.loc[idx, 'fdr'] = multipletests(pvals, method='fdr_bh')[1]
        # Pivot into the required wide format.
        wide_rows = []
        for (tp, conc, gene_set), grp in dna_out.groupby(['timepoint', 'concentration', 'gene_set'], sort=False):
            rec = {
                'dna_damage_result_id': f'{tp}|{conc}|{gene_set}',
                'timepoint': tp,
                'concentration': conc,
                'gene_set': gene_set,
2026-11-14T23:38:37.907604+00:00 · bash_session · { "action": "type_submit", "input": "sed -n '641,860p' /workspace/work/pathway_enrichment.py" }
{
  "action": "type_submit",
  "input": "sed -n '641,860p' /workspace/work/pathway_enrichment.py"
}

            }
            for direction in ['non_directional', 'up', 'down']:
                sub = grp[grp['direction'] == direction]
                if sub.empty:
                    rec[f'p_value_{direction}'] = 1.0
                    rec[f'fdr_{direction}'] = 1.0
                    rec[f'significant_in_gene_set_{direction}'] = 0
                else:
                    rec[f'p_value_{direction}'] = float(sub['p_value'].iloc[0])
                    rec[f'fdr_{direction}'] = float(sub['fdr'].iloc[0])
                    rec[f'significant_in_gene_set_{direction}'] = int(sub['significant_in_gene_set'].iloc[0])
            wide_rows.append(rec)
        dna_wide = pd.DataFrame(wide_rows)
    else:
        dna_wide = pd.DataFrame(columns=[
            'dna_damage_result_id', 'timepoint', 'concentration', 'gene_set',
            'p_value_non_directional', 'fdr_non_directional', 'significant_in_gene_set_non_directional',
            'p_value_up', 'fdr_up', 'significant_in_gene_set_up',
            'p_value_down', 'fdr_down', 'significant_in_gene_set_down',
        ])
    # Ensure the required columns and ordering.
    desired_cols = [
        'dna_damage_result_id', 'timepoint', 'concentration', 'gene_set',
        'p_value_non_directional', 'fdr_non_directional', 'significant_in_gene_set_non_directional',
        'p_value_up', 'fdr_up', 'significant_in_gene_set_up',
        'p_value_down', 'fdr_down', 'significant_in_gene_set_down',
    ]
    for col in desired_cols:
        if col not in dna_wide.columns:
            dna_wide[col] = np.nan if col.startswith('p_value') or col.startswith('fdr') else 0
    dna_wide = dna_wide[desired_cols]
    dna_wide = dna_wide.sort_values(['timepoint', 'concentration', 'gene_set'])
    dna_wide.to_csv(DNA_OUT, index=False)

    main()

#!/usr/bin/env Rscript
# Map mouse TempO-Seq manifest identifiers to human ortholog symbols.
#
# The analysis follows the benchmark guidance to query Ensembl release 100 via
# biomaRt, using the mouse gene symbols from the BioSpyder manifest as input.
# For mouse symbols with multiple human ortholog annotations, the preferred
# mapping is chosen by ordering orthology type (one-to-one first) and confidence
# (descending), then taking the first remaining hit.
#
# Output:
#   /workspace/work/probe_human_map.tsv

suppressPackageStartupMessages({
  library(data.table)
  library(biomaRt)
  library(dplyr)
})

manifest_path <- '/data/metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv'
out_path <- '/workspace/work/probe_human_map.tsv'

manifest <- fread(manifest_path)
mouse_symbols <- sort(unique(manifest$GENE_SYMBOL))
mouse_symbols <- mouse_symbols[!is.na(mouse_symbols) & mouse_symbols != '']

mart <- useEnsembl(biomart = 'genes', dataset = 'mmusculus_gene_ensembl', version = 100)
attrs <- c('mgi_symbol', 'hsapiens_homolog_associated_gene_name', 'hsapiens_homolog_orthology_type', 'hsapiens_homolog_orthology_confidence')
map <- getBM(attributes = attrs, filters = 'mgi_symbol', values = mouse_symbols, mart = mart)
if (nrow(map) == 0) stop('biomaRt returned no orthology mappings')

map <- map %>%
  mutate(
    orthology_confidence = as.integer(hsapiens_homolog_orthology_confidence),
    orthology_rank = case_when(
      hsapiens_homolog_orthology_type == 'ortholog_one2one' ~ 1L,
      hsapiens_homolog_orthology_type == 'ortholog_one2many' ~ 2L,
      hsapiens_homolog_orthology_type == 'many2many' ~ 3L,
      TRUE ~ 4L
    )
  ) %>%
  filter(!is.na(hsapiens_homolog_associated_gene_name) & hsapiens_homolog_associated_gene_name != '') %>%
  arrange(mgi_symbol, orthology_rank, desc(orthology_confidence), hsapiens_homolog_associated_gene_name) %>%
  group_by(mgi_symbol) %>%
  slice(1) %>%
  ungroup() %>%
  transmute(
    mouse_symbol = mgi_symbol,
    human_symbol = hsapiens_homolog_associated_gene_name,
    orthology_type = hsapiens_homolog_orthology_type,
    orthology_confidence = orthology_confidence
  )

probe_map <- manifest %>%
  select(PROBE_NAME, GENE_SYMBOL) %>%
  rename(gene_probe_id = PROBE_NAME, mouse_symbol = GENE_SYMBOL) %>%
  left_join(map, by = 'mouse_symbol') %>%
  arrange(gene_probe_id)

fwrite(probe_map, out_path, sep = '\t', quote = FALSE, na = 'NA')

summary <- data.frame(
  n_probes = nrow(probe_map),
  n_probes_mapped = sum(!is.na(probe_map$human_symbol)),
  n_unique_mouse_symbols = length(mouse_symbols),
  n_unique_mouse_symbols_mapped = length(unique(probe_map$mouse_symbol[!is.na(probe_map$human_symbol)]))
)
write.table(summary, '/workspace/work/probe_human_map.summary.tsv', sep = '\t', row.names = FALSE, quote = FALSE)
