The manifest `PROBE_SEQUENCE` values were written to a temporary FASTA in `work/temposeq_ref/temposeq_manifest_probes.fa` with each record labeled by `PROBE_NAME`, and Bowtie index files were generated in the same directory (`work/temposeq_ref/temposeq_manifest_probes_index.*`). Library IDs were matched to FASTQ files via the staged sample metadata (`data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv`) and ENA run metadata (`data/metadata/ena_run_metadata.tsv`), ensuring that the 85 required post-QC libraries were processed in the exact output-contract order. Each FASTQ was streamed through `pigz 2.6` and aligned with `bowtie -q -S --best --strata -m 1 -p 1`, which retains only uniquely reportable alignments; raw probe counts were extracted from the SAM stream with `awk` and written as sparse per-sample tables under `work/counts/by_sample/*.counts.tsv` together with Bowtie logs (`*.bowtie.log`) and a mapping summary (`work/counts/mapping_stats.tsv`). The initial sparse extraction emitted a small number of SAM-header tokens (`VN:1.3.1`, `SO:unsorted`, `LN:46`–`LN:50`); these were excluded during matrix assembly so that only manifest probe names remained. The final dense probe-by-sample count matrix was written as unnormalized integer counts to `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv`.\n\n## deseq2_significant_degs\nThe count matrix from `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv` was analyzed in R 4.2.2 Patched using DESeq2 1.38.3, with sample annotations taken from `data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv`. Separate DESeq2 models were fit within each timepoint (day7, day28, and day90) using the design formula `~ treatment_ppm`, where `treatment_ppm` was treated as an ordered factor with levels `0ppm`, `40ppm`, `200ppm`, `600ppm`, `2000ppm`, and `6000ppm`. For each timepoint, Wald tests compared each nonzero concentration against the time-matched 0 ppm control. The full set of Wald-test results was written to `work/deseq2_all_results.csv`, and rows with Benjamini–Hochberg adjusted p-value < 0.10 were retained in `outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv`. Positive log2 fold changes indicate higher expression in treated mice relative to the matched control, and negative values indicate higher expression in controls. The result table preserves probe-level `PROBE_NAME` identifiers as the row key (`contrast_gene_id = timepoint|concentration|gene_probe_id`).\n\n## piano_preranked_gsea\nDirectional preranked gene-set enrichment was performed on DESeq2 Wald statistics after converting mouse probe-level identifiers to human ortholog symbols. Direct queries to the Ensembl BioMart archive (`biomaRt` 2.54.1 against Ensembl release 100) were not usable in this environment because the archive endpoint returned proxy HTTP 403 errors through the available squid gateway; therefore, the ortholog mapping was reproduced with `babelgene` 22.9 as a deterministic fallback. The mapping script (`work/build_mouse_human_mapping.R`) joined the manifest gene symbols to one preferred human ortholog per mouse symbol (`min_support = 3`, `top = TRUE`) and wrote the probe-level map to `work/probe_human_map.tsv` together with a mapping summary (`work/probe_human_map.summary.tsv`). For each timepoint/concentration contrast, one representative probe per human symbol was selected by maximum absolute Wald statistic (with deterministic tie-breaking) before preranked GSEA. The canonical pathway collections were the MSigDB v6.2 GMTs downloaded into `work/msigdb/` (`c2.cp.v6.2.symbols.gmt` plus `h.all.v6.2.symbols.gmt`), and gene sets were retained only if 5–500 mapped genes overlapped the contrast-specific human-gene universe. Because `piano` 2.4.0 and its `fgsea` dependency were not installable cleanly in the available R toolchain, preranked enrichment was implemented with `gseapy` 1.3.2 instead. The final runs used `gseapy.prerank` with 1,000 permutations, `seed = 1`, `min_size = 5`, `max_size = 500`, `no_plot = TRUE`, and 8 threads. A first provisional prerank pass emitted warnings about duplicated ranking statistics; to eliminate arbitrary tie handling, the script was patched to use a stable sort plus an infinitesimal deterministic tie-breaker and was rerun. Only gene sets with FDR < 0.10 were written to `outputs/piano_preranked_gsea.csv`; the direction label (`up` or `down`) was assigned from the sign of the normalized enrichment score.\n\n## hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment\nHypergeometric overrepresentation analysis was carried out on the same human-ortholog representation used for preranked GSEA, using the combined MSigDB v6.2 canonical pathway collection (`work/msigdb/c2.cp.v6.2.symbols.gmt` plus `work/msigdb/h.all.v6.2.symbols.gmt`). For each timepoint/concentration contrast, significant DE genes were defined at DESeq2 BH-adjusted p-value < 0.10 and separated into three sets: all significant genes, up-regulated significant genes, and down-regulated significant genes. The background universe for each contrast was the set of human symbols represented in the contrast-specific ranked list after ortholog mapping. One-sided hypergeometric p-values were computed with `scipy.stats.hypergeom` and adjusted within each contrast/direction by Benjamini–Hochberg using `statsmodels.stats.multitest.multipletests`. Only FDR < 0.10 rows were retained in `outputs/hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment.csv`. This analysis reuses the intermediate ortholog map (`work/probe_human_map.tsv`) and the full DESeq2 result table (`work/deseq2_all_results.csv`).\n\n## dna_damage_enrichment\nCurated DNA-damage and repair gene sets were parsed from the staged workbook `data/metadata/supplementary/mmc3_dna_damage_gene_sets.xlsx` with `openpyxl` 3.1.5. The workbook’s first sheet (`Table S4. DNA damage gene sets`) was read row-wise; the header row labeled `Name of Geneset` was skipped, gene names were taken from the remaining columns, and `GO_CELLULAR_RESPONSE_TO_DNA_DAMAGE_STIMULUS` was excluded, leaving 88 curated gene sets. The background universe for these analyses was the union of all genes appearing in the retained 88 sets. Hypergeometric overrepresentation was then evaluated for the 13 contrasts specified in the output contract: day7 600/2000/6000 ppm; day28 40/200/600/2000/6000 ppm; and day90 40/200/600/2000/6000 ppm. For each contrast, the same three significant-gene subsets used above were tested separately (non-directional, up-regulated, and down-regulated). P-values were BH-adjusted within each contrast/direction and the results were pivoted to a wide table with one row per timepoint/concentration/gene-set combination. Unlike the canonical-pathway outputs, these rows were not filtered by significance; the full 13 × 88 table was written to `outputs/dna_damage_enrichment.csv`. Intermediate inputs reused the DESeq2 results (`work/deseq2_all_results.csv`) and ortholog map (`work/probe_human_map.tsv`).\n"

# Methods

## tempo_seq_filtered_counts
Single-end TempO-Seq FASTQ files for the retained post-QC female B6D2F1/Crl mouse liver cohort were quantified against the BioSpyder mouse whole-transcriptome v1.1 manifest (`data/metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv`) using a Bowtie 1.3.1 alignment workflow that reproduces the unique-alignment behavior used by QuasR for simple assays. The manifest `PROBE_SEQUENCE` values were written to a temporary FASTA in `work/temposeq_ref/temposeq_manifest_probes.fa` with each record labeled by `PROBE_NAME`, and Bowtie index files were generated in the same directory (`work/temposeq_ref/temposeq_manifest_probes_index.*`). Library IDs were matched to FASTQ files via the staged sample metadata (`data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv`) and ENA run metadata (`data/metadata/ena_run_metadata.tsv`), ensuring that the 85 required post-QC libraries were processed in the exact output-contract order. Each FASTQ was streamed through `pigz 2.6` and aligned with `bowtie -q -S --best --strata -m 1 -p 1`, which retains only uniquely reportable alignments; raw probe counts were extracted from the SAM stream with `awk` and written as sparse per-sample tables under `work/counts/by_sample/*.counts.tsv` together with Bowtie logs (`*.bowtie.log`) and a mapping summary (`work/counts/mapping_stats.tsv`). The initial sparse extraction emitted a small number of SAM-header tokens (`VN:1.3.1`, `SO:unsorted`, `LN:46`–`LN:50`); these were excluded during matrix assembly so that only manifest probe names remained. The final dense probe-by-sample count matrix was written as unnormalized integer counts to `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv`.

## deseq2_significant_degs
The count matrix from `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv` was analyzed in R 4.2.2 Patched using DESeq2 1.38.3, with sample annotations taken from `data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv`. Separate DESeq2 models were fit within each timepoint (day7, day28, and day90) using the design formula `~ treatment_ppm`, where `treatment_ppm` was treated as an ordered factor with levels `0ppm`, `40ppm`, `200ppm`, `600ppm`, `2000ppm`, and `6000ppm`. For each timepoint, Wald tests compared each nonzero concentration against the time-matched 0 ppm control. The full set of Wald-test results was written to `work/deseq2_all_results.csv`, and rows with Benjamini–Hochberg adjusted p-value < 0.10 were retained in `outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv`. Positive log2 fold changes indicate higher expression in treated mice relative to the matched control, and negative values indicate higher expression in controls. The result table preserves probe-level `PROBE_NAME` identifiers as the row key (`contrast_gene_id = timepoint|concentration|gene_probe_id`).

## piano_preranked_gsea
Directional preranked gene-set enrichment was performed on DESeq2 Wald statistics after converting mouse probe-level identifiers to human ortholog symbols. Direct queries to the Ensembl BioMart archive (`biomaRt` 2.54.1 against Ensembl release 100) were not usable in this environment because the archive endpoint returned proxy HTTP 403 errors through the available squid gateway; therefore, the ortholog mapping was reproduced with `babelgene` 22.9 as a deterministic fallback. The mapping script (`work/build_mouse_human_mapping.R`) joined the manifest gene symbols to one preferred human ortholog per mouse symbol (`min_support = 3`, `top = TRUE`) and wrote the probe-level map to `work/probe_human_map.tsv` together with a mapping summary (`work/probe_human_map.summary.tsv`). For each timepoint/concentration contrast, one representative probe per human symbol was selected by maximum absolute Wald statistic (with deterministic tie-breaking) before preranked GSEA. The canonical pathway collections were the MSigDB v6.2 GMTs downloaded into `work/msigdb/` (`c2.cp.v6.2.symbols.gmt` plus `h.all.v6.2.symbols.gmt`), and gene sets were retained only if 5–500 mapped genes overlapped the contrast-specific human-gene universe. Because `piano` 2.4.0 and its `fgsea` dependency were not installable cleanly in the available R toolchain, preranked enrichment was implemented with `gseapy` 1.3.2 instead. The final runs used `gseapy.prerank` with 1,000 permutations, `seed = 1`, `min_size = 5`, `max_size = 500`, `no_plot = TRUE`, and 8 threads. A first provisional prerank pass emitted warnings about duplicated ranking statistics; to eliminate arbitrary tie handling, the script was patched to use a stable sort plus an infinitesimal deterministic tie-breaker and was rerun. Only gene sets with FDR < 0.10 were written to `outputs/piano_preranked_gsea.csv`; the direction label (`up` or `down`) was assigned from the sign of the normalized enrichment score.

## hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment
Hypergeometric overrepresentation analysis was carried out on the same human-ortholog representation used for preranked GSEA, using the combined MSigDB v6.2 canonical pathway collection (`work/msigdb/c2.cp.v6.2.symbols.gmt` plus `work/msigdb/h.all.v6.2.symbols.gmt`). For each timepoint/concentration contrast, significant DE genes were defined at DESeq2 BH-adjusted p-value < 0.10 and separated into three sets: all significant genes, up-regulated significant genes, and down-regulated significant genes. The background universe for each contrast was the set of human symbols represented in the contrast-specific ranked list after ortholog mapping. One-sided hypergeometric p-values were computed with `scipy.stats.hypergeom` and adjusted within each contrast/direction by Benjamini–Hochberg using `statsmodels.stats.multitest.multipletests`. Only FDR < 0.10 rows were retained in `outputs/hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment.csv`. This analysis reuses the intermediate ortholog map (`work/probe_human_map.tsv`) and the full DESeq2 result table (`work/deseq2_all_results.csv`).

## dna_damage_enrichment
Curated DNA-damage and repair gene sets were parsed from the staged workbook `data/metadata/supplementary/mmc3_dna_damage_gene_sets.xlsx` with `openpyxl` 3.1.5. The workbook’s first sheet (`Table S4. DNA damage gene sets`) was read row-wise; the header row labeled `Name of Geneset` was skipped, gene names were taken from the remaining columns, and `GO_CELLULAR_RESPONSE_TO_DNA_DAMAGE_STIMULUS` was excluded, leaving 88 curated gene sets. The background universe for these analyses was the union of all genes appearing in the retained 88 sets. Hypergeometric overrepresentation was then evaluated for the 13 contrasts specified in the output contract: day7 600/2000/6000 ppm; day28 40/200/600/2000/6000 ppm; and day90 40/200/600/2000/6000 ppm. For each contrast, the same three significant-gene subsets used above were tested separately (non-directional, up-regulated, and down-regulated). P-values were BH-adjusted within each contrast/direction and the results were pivoted to a wide table with one row per timepoint/concentration/gene-set combination. Unlike the canonical-pathway outputs, these rows were not filtered by significance; the full 13 × 88 table was written to `outputs/dna_damage_enrichment.csv`. Intermediate inputs reused the DESeq2 results (`work/deseq2_all_results.csv`) and ortholog map (`work/probe_human_map.tsv`).

     6	## deseq2_significant_degs
     7	The count matrix from `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv` was analyzed in R 4.2.2 Patched using DESeq2 1.38.3, with sample annotations taken from `data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv`. Separate DESeq2 models were fit within each timepoint (day7, day28, and day90) using the design formula `~ treatment_ppm`, where `treatment_ppm` was treated as an ordered factor with levels `0ppm`, `40ppm`, `200ppm`, `600ppm`, `2000ppm`, and `6000ppm`. For each timepoint, Wald tests compared each nonzero concentration against the time-matched 0 ppm control. The full set of Wald-test results was written to `work/deseq2_all_results.csv`, and rows with Benjamini–Hochberg adjusted p-value < 0.10 were retained in `outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv`. Positive log2 fold changes indicate higher expression in treated mice relative to the matched control, and negative values indicate higher expression in controls. The result table preserves probe-level `PROBE_NAME` identifiers as the row key (`contrast_gene_id = timepoint|concentration|gene_probe_id`).

     9	## piano_preranked_gsea
    10	Directional preranked gene-set enrichment was performed on DESeq2 Wald statistics after converting mouse probe-level identifiers to human ortholog symbols. Direct queries to the Ensembl BioMart archive (`biomaRt` 2.54.1 against Ensembl release 100) were not usable in this environment because the archive endpoint returned proxy HTTP 403 errors through the available squid gateway; therefore, the ortholog mapping was reproduced with `babelgene` 22.9 as a deterministic fallback. The mapping script (`work/build_mouse_human_mapping.R`) joined the manifest gene symbols to one preferred human ortholog per mouse symbol (`min_support = 3`, `top = TRUE`) and wrote the probe-level map to `work/probe_human_map.tsv` together with a mapping summary (`work/probe_human_map.summary.tsv`). The downstream enrichment calculations were orchestrated by `work/pathway_enrichment.py`, and the final rerun was captured in `work/pathway_enrichment.log`. For each timepoint/concentration contrast, one representative probe per human symbol was selected by maximum absolute Wald statistic (with deterministic tie-breaking) before preranked GSEA. The canonical pathway collections were the MSigDB v6.2 GMTs downloaded into `work/msigdb/` (`c2.cp.v6.2.symbols.gmt` plus `h.all.v6.2.symbols.gmt`), and gene sets were retained only if 5–500 mapped genes overlapped the contrast-specific human-gene universe. The final runs used `gseapy.prerank` with 1,000 permutations, `seed = 1`, `min_size = 5`, `max_size = 500`, `no_plot = TRUE`, and 8 threads. A first provisional prerank pass emitted warnings about duplicated ranking statistics; to eliminate arbitrary tie handling, the script was patched to use a stable sort plus an infinitesimal deterministic tie-breaker and was rerun. Only gene sets with FDR < 0.10 were written to `outputs/piano_preranked_gsea.csv`; the direction label (`up` or `down`) was assigned from the sign of the normalized enrichment score.
